<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Bogoliubov quantum-depletion asymptotic for the ground state of a three-dimensional Bose gas with a fixed bounded, nonnegative, radial interaction of finite range and positive scattering length <i>a</i>. The thermodynamic limit is taken at each fixed density <i>ρ</i> before the dilute limit. Every thermodynamic accumulation value of the fraction outside the constant mode is $`\frac{8}{3\sqrt\pi}\sqrt{\rho a^3}+o(\sqrt{\rho a^3})`$ as $`\rho\downarrow0`$. The assertion is uniform over ground-state density matrices and does not require the occupation to have a unique thermodynamic limit.

- **claim-universal-ground-state-depletion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every potential $v$ satisfying the assumptions above and every $\epsilon>0$, there is $\rho_0(v,\epsilon)>0$ such that, for every fixed $0<\rho<\rho_0(v,\epsilon)$ and every sequence $N_k,L_k\to\infty$ with $N_k/L_k^3\to\rho$, $$\limsup_{k\to\infty}\ \sup_{\Gamma\in\mathcal G^v_{N_k,L_k}} \left\|\frac{1-B_\Gamma}{\sqrt{\rho a_v^3}} -\frac8{3\sqrt\pi}\right\|\le\epsilon.$$
  - Explanation: The constant-orbital depletion has the universal Bogoliubov leading term for a fixed bounded radial repulsive interaction, with the thermodynamic limit taken before dilution. The assertion covers every thermodynamic sequence without assuming an occupation limit.
- **claim-uniform-density-tail-input** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-density-input)): Let $v$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. There is a sufficiently large fixed $B_0$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\rho>0$, the following statements hold at physical base length $b=B_0\rho^{-1/2}$ and count scale $n=\rho b^3$.* 1. *If the particle density is at most $2\rho$, let $A=\sum_i g(x_i)$, where $0\le g\le1$, the support of $g$ is covered by $J$ base cubes, and $\|\nabla g\|^2\le C_gb^{-2}g$ almost everywhere for a prescribed fixed bound on $C_g$. Then $$\mathbb P(A>M)\le C e^{-cM},\qquad M\ge CnJ.$$ Sharp counts have such majorants after a fixed enlargement and a fixed increase in $J$.* 2. *If the density lies in $[\rho/2,2\rho]$ and $Q_1,\ldots,Q_J$ are cubes of side $b$ with centers separated by a sufficiently large fixed multiple of $b$, then $$\mathbb P\left(\sum_{i=1}^J n_{Q_i}\le\epsilon_0 nJ\right) \le e^{-c_1 nJ}$$ for fixed $\epsilon_0,c_1>0$.* *Both assertions hold in all sufficiently large volumes, uniformly down to arbitrarily small positive temperatures. Their volume thresholds do not grow as temperature decreases. Fixed positive multiplicative changes in cube sides are allowed after changing constants, and $B_0$ can be increased to meet any finite list of lower bounds.
  - Explanation: The imported upper and lower count tails are uniform as positive temperature decreases. This permits their passage to finite-volume ground states and their use along thermodynamic sequences.
- **claim-scaled-count-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-count-tails)): After fixing $B_0$ and decreasing $\rho$ if necessary, there are positive constants $c,c',C$ such that, for every nonempty $A\subset\mathcal L$, $$\label{eq:count-tails} \begin{aligned} \mathbb P_\mu\left(\sum_{z\in A}n_{U_z}\ge b\right) &\le C e^{-cb},&& b\ge CD\|A\|,\\ \mathbb P_\mu\left(n_{U_z}<c'D\text{ for every }z\in A\right) &\le e^{-cD\|A\|}. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Ground-state counts in arbitrary nonempty collections of scaled unit cells obey exponential upper tails and simultaneous low-count tails.
- **claim-free-path-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-path-domination)): If $m$ specified labels carry nonnegative single-path weights $H_i$ that are invariant under time reversal, then under <a href="#eq:ground-path-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ground-path-law">[eq:ground-path-law]</a>, $$\label{eq:path-domination} \mathbb E\prod_{i=1}^m H_i(\omega_i) \le e^{Cm}\int\mu(\,\mathrm dY) \prod_{i=1}^m\mathbb E^{Y_i}_{\mathrm{free}}H_i.$$ A weight without reversal symmetry can be replaced by the sum of itself and its reversal.
  - Explanation: Nonnegative reversal-invariant path weights under the interacting ground-state law are dominated by free-path expectations with the original position marginal. A nonsymmetric weight can be enlarged using its reversal.
- **claim-local-energy-and-occupation-outputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-local-energy)): Choose $u>0$ sufficiently small and then $0<w<u/10000$, with the partitions of Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">2</a>. In the stipulated order of thermodynamic and dilute limits, the ground vector satisfies $$\label{eq:local-energy} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_{A\in\mathcal C_R}\langle(q_A-1)^2\rangle_\Phi &\le CD^{-1-w/6},& \mathbb E_{A\in\mathcal C_d}\langle(q_A-1)^4\rangle_\Phi &\le C_jD^{-1-u/6},\\ V^{-1}\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(W_R)\rangle_\Phi &=\frac8{3\sqrt\pi}\alpha^{3/2}+o(1),& V^{-1}\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(W_d)\rangle_\Phi &\le\epsilon_j+o_j(1),\qquad \epsilon_j\longrightarrow0. \end{aligned}$$ The parameter $j$ is fixed before taking these limits. Constants are uniform for $\alpha$ in the fixed compact positive window.
  - Explanation: Local energy estimates control coarse count variance, fine count fourth moments, mesoscopic depletion, and the fine-scale occupation error. The fine partition parameter is fixed during the thermodynamic and dilute limits.
- **claim-rough-neumann-box-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-energy-rough)): For $0\le n\le6Dv_b$, as a quadratic-form inequality on the bosonic $n$-particle Neumann space, $$\label{eq:rough-energy} \begin{split} H_b\ge{}&\frac{4\pi an^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-0.001} +c\,\mathrm d\Gamma(\min\{p^2,1\})\\ &+cD\sum_{A\in\mathcal C_d,\ A\subset b}\|A\| \mathfrak h\left(q_A-\frac{n}{Dv_b}\right), \qquad \mathfrak h(y)=\frac{y^2}{1+\|y\|}. \end{split}$$ The same inequality holds with Hilbert-space-valued spectator variables.
  - Explanation: A rough Neumann lower bound retains kinetic and local-count penalties rather than only a scalar energy estimate. It also applies to vectors with spectator variables.
- **claim-sharp-box-positive-remainders** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-energy-sharp)): If $Dv_b/2\le n\le6Dv_b$ and $\psi$ is supported on $n_L\le M/4$, then $$\label{eq:sharp-energy} \begin{split} \langle H_b\rangle_\psi\ge{}& \bigl(v_be(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\bigr)\\|\psi\\|^2+c\langle S\rangle_\psi\\ &+c\int_{\|z\|^2\le2n}\sum_{p\ne0}\widetilde D_p \\|b_p\psi_z\\|^2\,\,\mathrm d\lambda(z),\\ &\hspace{12mm}\widetilde D_p=D_p\ \text{on the low band},\qquad \widetilde D_p=D_p/J\ \text{on the high band}. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The sharp lower bound matches the local energy scalar while retaining particle-excitation and quasiparticle forms. These positive remainders later control occupation observables.
- **claim-energy-surplus-controls-penalties** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-energy-surplus)): After summing boxes, assignment sectors, and spectator variables, each of the following quantities is bounded by $CVQ^{-1/5}$:* 1. *the original box-count penalty $\sum_bDv_b\langle\mathfrak h(n/(Dv_b)-1)\rangle$;* 2. *on high pieces, the sum of $\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(\min\{p^2,1\})\rangle$ and $D\sum_{A\subset b}\|A\|\langle\mathfrak h(q_A-1)\rangle$;* 3. *on low pieces, the $S$ form and the integrated positive quasiparticle form in <a href="#eq:sharp-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sharp-energy">[eq:sharp-energy]</a>;* 4. *on number sectors outside $[Dv_b/2,6Dv_b]$, the sum of $(n+Dv_b)$ times their squared norms.
  - Explanation: After all spatial and particle-sector integrations, the available energy surplus pays for the original count penalty, high-piece penalties, low-piece positive forms, and outlying number sectors.
- **claim-exact-conditional-soft-path-law** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-path-law)): For almost every exposed datum $\mathcal D$, the conditional law of the paths with labels in $I$ is $$\label{eq:conditional-path-law} \frac1{Z(\mathcal D)}\prod_{i\in I}J_i(\omega_i) \prod_{\substack{i<k\\\{i,k\}\cap I\ne\varnothing}} \mathbf 1_{\{u_{ik}\le a_{ik}(\omega)\}} \prod_{i\in I}B_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i), \qquad Z(\mathcal D)>0.$$ Frozen paths in this formula are held at their exposed values. Every pair indicator can fail only if the two paths approach within distance $r$ at some time.
  - Explanation: Exposing the auxiliary interaction variables produces an exact indicator-weighted product-bridge law on each realized conditional fiber. A failed pair check requires a close approach within the interaction range.
- **claim-good-data-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-good-data)): The constants in the preceding definitions can be chosen so that $$\label{eq:good-prob} \begin{aligned} \mathbb P(z\text{ is bad})&\le C_xD^{-1-1/8},\\ \mathbb P(\text{every site in }A\text{ is bad})&\le p^{\|A\|} &&(A\subset\mathcal L),\\ \mathbb P(\text{full-path bounds fail in a fixed neighborhood}) &\le C_xD^{-6}. \end{aligned}$$ In the second line $p>0$ may be any sufficiently small fixed number, after increasing the threshold for $D$. The constants and thresholds are uniform in the position of the neighborhood and in the volume.
  - Explanation: The good-site tests can be chosen to give a small single-site failure probability, a joint bad-set bound, and a stronger failure bound for the full-path event. The thresholds are uniform in location and volume.
- **claim-bridge-collision-costs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-collision-cost)): Fix a trial label $i$ with eligible endpoints and a distinct opponent $k$ satisfying the motion cutoff. The $B_i$-probability that the trial satisfies its individual cutoffs and comes within $r$ of the opponent is at most $C\beta_{ik}$. The same holds for a trial pinned at a point $y$ within $8b_0$ of $m_i$, provided $d_K(y,\omega_k(0))>s_1$, with the additional cost $$\label{eq:midpoint-cost} m(y,\omega_k)= \frac{b_D}{s_1\vee d_K(y,\omega_k(0))}.$$ If instead $y$ is uniform in a unit cell at an absolute bounded distance from $m_i$, entirely within this pin range, the integrated cost is at most $C_x\beta_{ik}$, including failure of the midpoint separation condition. These bounds also bound failures of <a href="#eq:soft-check" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:soft-check">[eq:soft-check]</a>, uniformly in its exposed auxiliary variable.
  - Explanation: A trial bridge collision is controlled by endpoint-distance costs. Prescribing a midpoint adds a midpoint-distance cost, while uniform midpoint averaging also covers separation failures. The estimates are uniform in the exposed interaction variables.
- **claim-uniform-insertion-at-good-data** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-insertion)): Fix exposed data for which the sites involved are good, with all required neighborhoods included in their tests. The following bounds hold uniformly after deletion of any subset of pair checks.* 1. *A $B_i$ trial with label $i\in I$ satisfies its individual cutoffs and all imposed checks against frozen paths and specified other labels of $I$ with probability at least $3/4$. The other unexposed paths may have any values satisfying their individual cutoffs.* 2. *The same bound holds when one first chooses a uniform midpoint in a unit cell at an absolute bounded distance from the trial mean, entirely within pin range, and then chooses the two independent bridge legs. Midpoint separation by $s_1$ from every opponent may be imposed as an additional test. For two such trials in different copies, using the same uniform midpoint and conditionally independent legs, joint success in their respective copies has probability at least $1/2$.* 3. *Suppose the opponents are paths from the original sample satisfying the full-path bounds in the relevant neighborhood. A trial pinned at any point in its pin range, separated by $s_1$ from all opponent midpoints, has success probability at least $3/4$, provided its endpoints have the stated separations. This remains true after removing opponents or adding or replacing boundedly many relevant paths satisfying the individual cutoffs and these separations.* *No assertion requires checks among the specified opponent paths to hold. Only their individual cutoffs and the stated data bounds are used.
  - Explanation: At good data, a trial has uniformly positive insertion probability against allowed opponents. The statement includes shared-midpoint trials and pins against the original collection, and remains valid when checks are deleted.
- **claim-product-free-bridge-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-bridge-domination)): Take finitely many labels at good sites, with product reference law $\prod_iB_i$, individual cutoffs, and any selection of the pair checks just considered. Normalize their indicator-weighted law. Its marginal on any $q$ labels has density at most $C^q$ relative to their product free-bridge law. The same conclusion holds conditionally on any individually allowed values of the other paths for which the conditional law is defined.
  - Explanation: Successive insertion bounds dominate selected path marginals by their product free-bridge law, including conditional marginals with individually allowed outside paths.
- **claim-flagged-count-moments-and-tag-omission** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-counts)): The local energy estimates imply $$\label{eq:flag-moments} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_{v\in\mathcal C_R}\mathbb E\|A_v-1\|^2 &\le C\bigl(D^{-1-w/6}+D^{-1+x}R^{-3}\bigr),\\ \mathbb E_{v\in\mathcal C_d}\mathbb E\|A_v-1\|^4 &\le C_j\bigl(D^{-1-u/6}+D^{-2+2x}\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ Let $$M_v'=\frac1{T_v}\sum_{i\text{ flagged},\ i\notin I}h_{vi}$$ be the relative count discarded when only tags in $I$ are retained. Uniformly over cells of either size, $$\label{eq:tag-omission} \mathbb EM_v'\le C_{x,j}D^{-1-1/8}.$$ In expectations involving a fixed number of cells, each additional relative-count factor can be bounded by $C_jD^x$, at an error smaller than any prescribed power of $D^{-1}$. The same assertion holds for fixed products of such factors.
  - Explanation: The local energy estimates and independent flagging yield coarse second moments and fine fourth moments for normalized flagged counts. Restricting tags to eligible flagged labels loses little mass, and count tails control additional relative-count factors.
- **claim-centered-local-likelihood** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-local-likelihood)): Include the second tag indicator but omit its divisor $T_{w'}$. On admissible output paths the left likelihood is $$\label{eq:local-likelihood} L_S(z;\eta)=\int c_S(o;\eta)h_{w'i_m}(o) k_S(o,\eta,z)B_S(\,\mathrm do).$$ It obeys $$\label{eq:local-centering} F_S=L_S-h_{w'i_m},\qquad \int F_S\,\,\mathrm d\lambda_S(\cdot\mid\eta)=0,$$ and $$\label{eq:local-likelihood-bound} 0\le L_S\le C_xD^{C\sqrt x}.$$ These assertions remain valid after deleting specified outside checks consistently from the reference, the original-path integral, and all insertion tests and their normalizing probabilities.
  - Explanation: The conditional normalizer cancels in the transported likelihood. Subtracting the terminal tag indicator gives exact conditional centering; a density bound survives consistent deletion of outside checks.
- **claim-local-switch-difference-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-switches)): Suppose the four changed sets are either pairwise disjoint, or have exactly one independent label identification. Write $\Delta_e$ for the finite difference from $\sigma_e=0$ to $\sigma_e=1$. Uniformly in all other switch settings, $$\label{eq:local-one-switch} \frac{\|\Delta_eG\|}{Z_U} \le C_xD^{C\sqrt x}\sum_{(l,k)\in\mathcal Q_e}\beta_{lk}.$$ If the changed sets are pairwise disjoint and $e\ne f$, then $$\label{eq:local-two-switches} \frac{\|\Delta_e\Delta_fG\|}{Z_U} \le C_xD^{C\sqrt x} \sum_{\substack{(l,k)\in\mathcal Q_e\\ (l',k')\in\mathcal Q_f}} \beta_{lk}\beta_{l'k'}.$$ The sums have at most $C_x$ terms. Moreover $\beta_{lk}\le C_xD^{-19/100}$ for sufficiently large $D$.
  - Explanation: A switched interaction forces a collision cost. For disjoint changed sets, distinct switches yield a product of collision costs; the weaker single-switch estimate also covers an independent label identification.
- **claim-adjacent-deletion-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-local-palm)): For sufficiently small fixed $x>0$, depending on $u$, the common measure constructed above satisfies $$\label{eq:local-lift-fourth} \mathbb E_{v\sim w'}\left[ \int\|\ell_v-1\|^4\,\,\mathrm dP_{\mathrm{aug},v} +\int\|\ell_{w'}-1\|^4\,\,\mathrm dP_{\mathrm{aug},w'}\right] \le C_jD^{-1-u/8}.$$ Consequently the local comparison <a href="#eq:local-fourth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-fourth">[eq:local-fourth]</a> holds. That bound, with a fixed change of constant, also holds when only adjacent pairs inside the same $R$-cell are retained in the average.
  - Explanation: The common transported measure has small fourth-moment likelihood errors relative to both augmented references. Projection gives the adjacent-cell square-root density comparison, including the restriction to edges within coarse cells.
- **claim-routes-with-rare-distant-encounters** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-route-input)): On $\mathcal L_K=(\mathbb Z/K\mathbb Z)^3$, let $d_\infty$ be the periodic maximum distance. A random datum $\mathcal D$ determines a bad set $\mathcal B\subset\mathcal L_K$ satisfying $$\mathbb P(A\subset\mathcal B)\le p^{\|A\|}\qquad(A\subset\mathcal L_K).$$ Fix an integer contact radius $r_c\ge1$, an integer $q\ge0$, and $c_Q>0$. Let $Q$ be a projected lattice cube of side at most $c_QR$, and let $z$ be a site such that every $y\in Q$ has forward first-coordinate displacement from $z$ in $[.29K,.46K]$. There are constants $p_0,\kappa,c,C,C_0>0$ and $R_0$, depending only on the fixed parameters, with the following properties when $R\ge R_0$, $K\ge C_0R$, and $p\le p_0$.* *Put $H=\lfloor R^{1/2}\rfloor$. There is an event $\mathcal E$, determined by $\mathcal D,z,Q$, on which one can sample simple good nearest-neighbor routes $\pi_y$ from $z$ to every $y\in Q$. Conditional on $\mathcal D$, different samples use independent fresh randomness. The event $\mathcal E$ includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.* *For $\pi=(v_0,\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, put $$a(\pi)=\max\{i:d_\infty(v_i,z)\le H\},\qquad \pi^{\rm ch}=\{v_i:i>a(\pi)\}.$$ The initial portion $(v_0,\ldots,v_{a(\pi)})$ has at most $CH$ vertices. The analogous portion read backwards from the terminal site also has at most $CH$ vertices, and these two portions are disjoint. Let $\pi^{\rm ch,q}$ contain the vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\pi^{\rm ch}$. For independent samples $\pi_y,\pi_{y'}$, set $$J_c=\sum_{v\in\pi_y}\sum_{v'\in\pi_{y'}} \mathbf 1_{\{d_\infty(v,v')\le r_c\}},\qquad J_*=\sum_{v\in\pi_y^{\rm ch,q}} \sum_{v'\in\pi_{y'}^{\rm ch,q}} \mathbf 1_{\{d_\infty(v,v')\le r_c\}}.$$ Uniformly in the specified endpoints, $$\label{eq:route-exponential} \mathbb E_{\mathcal D}\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E} \mathbb E_{\rm routes}(e^{\kappa J_c}\mid\mathcal D)\bigr]\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\omega$ on $Q\times Q$ with $\omega(y,y')\le C_\omega R^{-6}$, $$\label{eq:route-rare} \sum_{y,y'\in Q}\omega(y,y') \mathbb E_{\mathcal D}\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E} \mathbb P_{\rm routes}(J_*>0\mid\mathcal D)\bigr] \le CC_\omega R^{-c_g},\qquad c_g=\frac12\left(\frac{\log2}{\log3}-\frac12\right)>0.$$ Both estimates hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the same route construction in reverse order. Finally, if $\mathcal M$ is the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\label{eq:route-rejection} \mathbb P(\mathcal M\setminus\mathcal E) \le K^3(Ap)^{\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\rfloor}+CR^3e^{-cR},$$ where $A$ is fixed and $Ap_0<1$.
  - Explanation: Under the joint bad-set bound, a retained event supports sampling good simple routes. Their full contact count has an exponential moment, while contacts between changed portions become rare after endpoint averaging. The statement includes both route orientations and rejection control.
- **claim-long-transport-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-long-mass)): The common measure just constructed has mass satisfying $$\label{eq:long-mass} \operatorname{mass}\ge \mathbb E[A_vA_{w'}]-CD^{-1-c'}-o_K(1)$$ for some $c'>0$ and sufficiently small fixed $w,x$.
  - Explanation: Retaining the end portions and using subprobability pin kernels produces a common bath measure whose mass is close to the original tag-product mass.
- **claim-long-conditional-likelihood-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-long-product)): Conditional on $\mathcal D$ and the paths outside $U=S\cup T$, the two likelihoods satisfy $$\begin{aligned} S\cap T=\varnothing:\quad \mathbb E[L_SL_T\mid U^c] &\le \mathbb E[h_{w'j_1}h_{w'j_2}\mid U^c] +Ce^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c} \bigl(B_{ST}+C_xJ_*b_D(1+t^{-3/2})\bigr), \label{eq:long-product}\\ \|S\cap T\|=q\ge1:\quad \mathbb E[L_SL_T\mid U^c] &\le e^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}G_D^q. \label{eq:long-product-overlap} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Disjoint changed sets preserve the original tag-product leading term with an error charged to cross-route checks. Overlapping sets incur a density expense only for shared output labels.
- **claim-cross-check-normalizer-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-normalizer-comparison)): For these conditional reference laws, $$\label{eq:long-normalizer} \frac{Z_S^-Z_T^-}{Z_U} \le C^{\|S\cap T\|}e^{CB_{ST}}.$$ If $S\cap T=\varnothing$, then for any measurable $0\le F\le1$ of the latent originals, $$\label{eq:reference-difference} 0\le \frac1{Z_U}\int F(A_S^-A_T^--c_U)\,\,\mathrm dB_U \le CB_{ST}e^{CB_{ST}}.$$
  - Explanation: Restoring removed cross checks controls the ratio of separate normalizers to the union normalizer. In the disjoint case, bounded latent observables also admit a controlled reference-measure difference.
- **claim-distant-pair-spectral-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-distant-gap)): Let $M$ tend to infinity through integers, and let $(f_z)_{z\in(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)^3}$ be an array in a Hilbert space. Average pairs $(z,z+h)$ uniformly over $z$, over all transverse displacements, and over first-coordinate displacements $h_1$ in $[.30M,.45M]$. For all sufficiently large $M$, $$\mathbb E_z\\|f_z-\bar f\\|^2\le C\mathbb E_{z,h}\\|f_{z+h}-f_z\\|^2$$ with an absolute constant $C$.
  - Explanation: Averaging transverse displacements and a macroscopic interval of longitudinal displacements controls the variance of any Hilbert-valued array with a volume-independent constant.
- **claim-finite-array-rms-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rms-comparison)): For a finite nonnegative array $(y_i)$, with uniform independent indices $i,i'$, one has $$\label{eq:rms-fourth-inequality} \left(\sqrt{\mathbb E_i y_i^2}-\mathbb E_i y_i\right)^2 \le\frac12\mathbb E_{i,i'} \frac{\|y_i-y_{i'}\|^4}{y_i^2+y_{i'}^2},$$ where the quotient is zero when its denominator vanishes. Moreover, $$\label{eq:square-root-quotient} \|\sqrt y-\sqrt z\|^4\le\frac{\|y-z\|^4}{y^2+z^2} \le8\|\sqrt y-\sqrt z\|^4\qquad(y,z\ge0).$$
  - Explanation: For a nonnegative finite array, the discrepancy between its root mean square and arithmetic mean is controlled by weighted fourth differences. The companion square-root comparison allows these differences to be telescoped along cell paths.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-count-tails",
        "exact_quote": "After fixing $B_0$ and decreasing $\\rho$ if necessary, there are positive constants $c,c',C$ such that, for every nonempty $A\\subset\\mathcal L$, $$\\label{eq:count-tails}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(\\sum_{z\\in A}n_{U_z}\\ge b\\right)\n   &\\le C e^{-cb},&& b\\ge CD|A|,\\\\\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(n_{U_z}<c'D\\text{ for every }z\\in A\\right)\n   &\\le e^{-cD|A|}.\n \\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-count-tails",
          "exact_quote": "After fixing $B_0$ and decreasing $\\rho$ if necessary, there are positive constants $c,c',C$ such that, for every nonempty $A\\subset\\mathcal L$, $$\\label{eq:count-tails}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(\\sum_{z\\in A}n_{U_z}\\ge b\\right)\n   &\\le C e^{-cb},&& b\\ge CD|A|,\\\\\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(n_{U_z}<c'D\\text{ for every }z\\in A\\right)\n   &\\le e^{-cD|A|}.\n \\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "After fixing $B_0$ and decreasing $\\rho$ if necessary, there are positive constants $c,c',C$ such that, for every nonempty $A\\subset\\mathcal L$, $$\\label{eq:count-tails}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(\\sum_{z\\in A}n_{U_z}\\ge b\\right)\n   &\\le C e^{-cb},&& b\\ge CD|A|,\\\\\n \\mathbb P_\\mu\\left(n_{U_z}<c'D\\text{ for every }z\\in A\\right)\n   &\\le e^{-cD|A|}.\n \\end{aligned}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-domination",
        "exact_quote": "If $m$ specified labels carry nonnegative single-path weights $H_i$ that are invariant under time reversal, then under <a href=\"#eq:ground-path-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ground-path-law\">[eq:ground-path-law]</a>, $$\\label{eq:path-domination}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m H_i(\\omega_i)\n \\le e^{Cm}\\int\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dY)\n                  \\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E^{Y_i}_{\\mathrm{free}}H_i.$$ A weight without reversal symmetry can be replaced by the sum of itself and its reversal.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-domination",
          "exact_quote": "If $m$ specified labels carry nonnegative single-path weights $H_i$ that are invariant under time reversal, then under <a href=\"#eq:ground-path-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ground-path-law\">[eq:ground-path-law]</a>, $$\\label{eq:path-domination}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m H_i(\\omega_i)\n \\le e^{Cm}\\int\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dY)\n                  \\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E^{Y_i}_{\\mathrm{free}}H_i.$$ A weight without reversal symmetry can be replaced by the sum of itself and its reversal.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $m$ specified labels carry nonnegative single-path weights $H_i$ that are invariant under time reversal, then under <a href=\"#eq:ground-path-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ground-path-law\">[eq:ground-path-law]</a>, $$\\label{eq:path-domination}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m H_i(\\omega_i)\n \\le e^{Cm}\\int\\mu(\\,\\mathrm dY)\n                  \\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E^{Y_i}_{\\mathrm{free}}H_i.$$ A weight without reversal symmetry can be replaced by the sum of itself and its reversal.",
      "explanation": "Nonnegative reversal-invariant path weights under the interacting ground-state law are dominated by free-path expectations with the original position marginal. A nonsymmetric weight can be enlarged using its reversal.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-rough",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\le n\\le6Dv_b$, as a quadratic-form inequality on the bosonic $n$-particle Neumann space, $$\\label{eq:rough-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n H_b\\ge{}&\\frac{4\\pi an^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-0.001}\n       +c\\,\\mathrm d\\Gamma(\\min\\{p^2,1\\})\\\\\n &+cD\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_d,\\ A\\subset b}|A|\n    \\mathfrak h\\left(q_A-\\frac{n}{Dv_b}\\right),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak h(y)=\\frac{y^2}{1+|y|}.\n\\end{split}$$ The same inequality holds with Hilbert-space-valued spectator variables.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-rough",
          "exact_quote": "For $0\\le n\\le6Dv_b$, as a quadratic-form inequality on the bosonic $n$-particle Neumann space, $$\\label{eq:rough-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n H_b\\ge{}&\\frac{4\\pi an^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-0.001}\n       +c\\,\\mathrm d\\Gamma(\\min\\{p^2,1\\})\\\\\n &+cD\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_d,\\ A\\subset b}|A|\n    \\mathfrak h\\left(q_A-\\frac{n}{Dv_b}\\right),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak h(y)=\\frac{y^2}{1+|y|}.\n\\end{split}$$ The same inequality holds with Hilbert-space-valued spectator variables.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $0\\le n\\le6Dv_b$, as a quadratic-form inequality on the bosonic $n$-particle Neumann space, $$\\label{eq:rough-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n H_b\\ge{}&\\frac{4\\pi an^2}{v_b}-Cv_bD^{1-0.001}\n       +c\\,\\mathrm d\\Gamma(\\min\\{p^2,1\\})\\\\\n &+cD\\sum_{A\\in\\mathcal C_d,\\ A\\subset b}|A|\n    \\mathfrak h\\left(q_A-\\frac{n}{Dv_b}\\right),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak h(y)=\\frac{y^2}{1+|y|}.\n\\end{split}$$ The same inequality holds with Hilbert-space-valued spectator variables.",
      "explanation": "A rough Neumann lower bound retains kinetic and local-count penalties rather than only a scalar energy estimate. It also applies to vectors with spectator variables.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-sharp",
        "exact_quote": "If $Dv_b/2\\le n\\le6Dv_b$ and $\\psi$ is supported on $n_L\\le M/4$, then $$\\label{eq:sharp-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n \\langle H_b\\rangle_\\psi\\ge{}&\n \\bigl(v_be(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\\bigr)\\|\\psi\\|^2+c\\langle S\\rangle_\\psi\\\\\n &+c\\int_{|z|^2\\le2n}\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p\n                       \\|b_p\\psi_z\\|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda(z),\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\widetilde D_p=D_p\\ \\text{on the low band},\\qquad\n \\widetilde D_p=D_p/J\\ \\text{on the high band}.\n\\end{split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-sharp",
          "exact_quote": "If $Dv_b/2\\le n\\le6Dv_b$ and $\\psi$ is supported on $n_L\\le M/4$, then $$\\label{eq:sharp-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n \\langle H_b\\rangle_\\psi\\ge{}&\n \\bigl(v_be(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\\bigr)\\|\\psi\\|^2+c\\langle S\\rangle_\\psi\\\\\n &+c\\int_{|z|^2\\le2n}\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p\n                       \\|b_p\\psi_z\\|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda(z),\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\widetilde D_p=D_p\\ \\text{on the low band},\\qquad\n \\widetilde D_p=D_p/J\\ \\text{on the high band}.\n\\end{split}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $Dv_b/2\\le n\\le6Dv_b$ and $\\psi$ is supported on $n_L\\le M/4$, then $$\\label{eq:sharp-energy}\n\\begin{split}\n \\langle H_b\\rangle_\\psi\\ge{}&\n \\bigl(v_be(n/v_b)-Cv_bQ^{-1/4}\\bigr)\\|\\psi\\|^2+c\\langle S\\rangle_\\psi\\\\\n &+c\\int_{|z|^2\\le2n}\\sum_{p\\ne0}\\widetilde D_p\n                       \\|b_p\\psi_z\\|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda(z),\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\widetilde D_p=D_p\\ \\text{on the low band},\\qquad\n \\widetilde D_p=D_p/J\\ \\text{on the high band}.\n\\end{split}$$",
      "explanation": "The sharp lower bound matches the local energy scalar while retaining particle-excitation and quasiparticle forms. These positive remainders later control occupation observables.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-surplus",
        "exact_quote": "After summing boxes, assignment sectors, and spectator variables, each of the following quantities is bounded by $CVQ^{-1/5}$:*\n\n1.  *the original box-count penalty $\\sum_bDv_b\\langle\\mathfrak h(n/(Dv_b)-1)\\rangle$;*\n\n2.  *on high pieces, the sum of $\\langle\\mathrm d\\Gamma(\\min\\{p^2,1\\})\\rangle$ and $D\\sum_{A\\subset b}|A|\\langle\\mathfrak h(q_A-1)\\rangle$;*\n\n3.  *on low pieces, the $S$ form and the integrated positive quasiparticle form in <a href=\"#eq:sharp-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sharp-energy\">[eq:sharp-energy]</a>;*\n\n4.  *on number sectors outside $[Dv_b/2,6Dv_b]$, the sum of $(n+Dv_b)$ times their squared norms.",

      "exact_statement": "The constants in the preceding definitions can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:good-prob}\n\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P(z\\text{ is bad})&\\le C_xD^{-1-1/8},\\\\\n \\mathbb P(\\text{every site in }A\\text{ is bad})&\\le p^{|A|}\n                 &&(A\\subset\\mathcal L),\\\\\n \\mathbb P(\\text{full-path bounds fail in a fixed neighborhood})\n                  &\\le C_xD^{-6}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ In the second line $p>0$ may be any sufficiently small fixed number, after increasing the threshold for $D$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-collision-cost",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a trial label $i$ with eligible endpoints and a distinct opponent $k$ satisfying the motion cutoff. The $B_i$-probability that the trial satisfies its individual cutoffs and comes within $r$ of the opponent is at most $C\\beta_{ik}$. The same holds for a trial pinned at a point $y$ within $8b_0$ of $m_i$, provided $d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))>s_1$, with the additional cost $$\\label{eq:midpoint-cost}\n                   m(y,\\omega_k)=\n                       \\frac{b_D}{s_1\\vee d_K(y,\\omega_k(0))}.$$ If instead $y$ is uniform in a unit cell at an absolute bounded distance from $m_i$, entirely within this pin range, the integrated cost is at most $C_x\\beta_{ik}$, including failure of the midpoint separation condition. These bounds also bound failures of <a href=\"#eq:soft-check\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:soft-check\">[eq:soft-check]</a>, uniformly in its exposed auxiliary variable.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },

These bounds also bound failures of <a href=\"#eq:soft-check\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:soft-check\">[eq:soft-check]</a>, uniformly in its exposed auxiliary variable.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix exposed data for which the sites involved are good, with all required neighborhoods included in their tests. The following bounds hold uniformly after deletion of any subset of pair checks.*\n\n1.  *A $B_i$ trial with label $i\\in I$ satisfies its individual cutoffs and all imposed checks against frozen paths and specified other labels of $I$ with probability at least $3/4$. The other unexposed paths may have any values satisfying their individual cutoffs.*\n\n2.  *The same bound holds when one first chooses a uniform midpoint in a unit cell at an absolute bounded distance from the trial mean, entirely within pin range, and then chooses the two independent bridge legs. Midpoint separation by $s_1$ from every opponent may be imposed as an additional test. For two such trials in different copies, using the same uniform midpoint and conditionally independent legs, joint success in their respective copies has probability at least $1/2$.*\n\n3.  *Suppose the opponents are paths from the original sample satisfying the full-path bounds in the relevant neighborhood. A trial pinned at any point in its pin range, separated by $s_1$ from all opponent midpoints, has success probability at least $3/4$, provided its endpoints have the stated separations. This remains true after removing opponents or adding or replacing boundedly many relevant paths satisfying the individual cutoffs and these separations.*\n\n*No assertion requires checks among the specified opponent paths to hold. Only their individual cutoffs and the stated data bounds are used.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-insertion",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-counts",
        "exact_quote": "The local energy estimates imply $$\\label{eq:flag-moments}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_{v\\in\\mathcal C_R}\\mathbb E|A_v-1|^2\n   &\\le C\\bigl(D^{-1-w/6}+D^{-1+x}R^{-3}\\bigr),\\\\\n \\mathbb E_{v\\in\\mathcal C_d}\\mathbb E|A_v-1|^4\n   &\\le C_j\\bigl(D^{-1-u/6}+D^{-2+2x}\\bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Let $$M_v'=\\frac1{T_v}\\sum_{i\\text{ flagged},\\ i\\notin I}h_{vi}$$ be the relative count discarded when only tags in $I$ are retained. Uniformly over cells of either size, $$\\label{eq:tag-omission}\n                    \\mathbb EM_v'\\le C_{x,j}D^{-1-1/8}.$$ In expectations involving a fixed number of cells, each additional relative-count factor can be bounded by $C_jD^x$, at an error smaller than any prescribed power of $D^{-1}$. The same assertion holds for fixed products of such factors.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Include the second tag indicator but omit its divisor $T_{w'}$.         }

      "exact_statement": "For sufficiently small fixed $x>0$, depending on $u$, the common measure constructed above satisfies $$\\label{eq:local-lift-fourth}\n \\mathbb E_{v\\sim w'}\\left[\n       \\int|\\ell_v-1|^4\\,\\,\\mathrm dP_{\\mathrm{aug},v}\n       +\\int|\\ell_{w'}-1|^4\\,\\,\\mathrm dP_{\\mathrm{aug},w'}\\right]\n                         \\le C_jD^{-1-u/8}.$$ Consequently the local comparison <a href=\"#eq:local-fourth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:local-fourth\">[eq:local-fourth]</a> holds. That bound, with a fixed change of constant, also holds when only adjacent pairs inside the same $R$-cell are retained in the average.",
      "explanation": "The common transported measure has small fourth-moment likelihood errors relative to both augmented references. Projection gives the adjacent-cell square-root density comparison, including the restriction to edges within coarse cells.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-route-input",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal L_K=(\\mathbb Z/K\\mathbb Z)^3$, let $d_\\infty$ be the periodic maximum distance. A random datum $\\mathcal D$ determines a bad set $\\mathcal B\\subset\\mathcal L_K$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P(A\\subset\\mathcal B)\\le p^{|A|}\\qquad(A\\subset\\mathcal L_K).$$ Fix an integer contact radius $r_c\\ge1$, an integer $q\\ge0$, and $c_Q>0$. Let $Q$ be a projected lattice cube of side at most $c_QR$, and let $z$ be a site such that every $y\\in Q$ has forward first-coordinate displacement from $z$ in $[.29K,.46K]$. There are constants $p_0,\\kappa,c,C,C_0>0$ and $R_0$, depending only on the fixed parameters, with the following properties when $R\\ge R_0$, $K\\ge C_0R$, and $p\\le p_0$.*\n\n*Put $H=\\lfloor R^{1/2}\\rfloor$. There is an event $\\mathcal E$, determined by $\\mathcal D,z,Q$, on which one can sample simple good nearest-neighbor routes $\\pi_y$ from $z$ to every $y\\in Q$. Conditional on $\\mathcal D$, different samples use independent fresh randomness. The event $\\mathcal E$ includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.*\n\n\n\n\n*For $\\pi=(v_0,\\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, put $$a(\\pi)=\\max\\{i:d_\\infty(v_i,z)\\le H\\},\\qquad\n \\pi^{\\rm ch}=\\{v_i:i>a(\\pi)\\}.$$ The initial portion $(v_0,\\ldots,v_{a(\\pi)})$ has at most $CH$ vertices. The analogous portion read backwards from the terminal site also has at most $CH$ vertices, and these two portions are disjoint. Let $\\pi^{\\rm ch,q}$ contain the vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\\pi^{\\rm ch}$. For independent samples $\\pi_y,\\pi_{y'}$, set $$J_c=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y}\\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}},\\qquad\n J_*=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}}.$$ Uniformly in the specified endpoints, $$\\label{eq:route-exponential}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\bigl[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E}\n       \\mathbb E_{\\rm routes}(e^{\\kappa J_c}\\mid\\mathcal D)\\bigr]\\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\\omega$ on $Q\\times Q$ with $\\omega(y,y')\\le C_\\omega R^{-6}$, $$\\label{eq:route-rare}\n \\sum_{y,y'\\in Q}\\omega(y,y')\n   \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\bigl[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E}\n       \\mathbb P_{\\rm routes}(J_*>0\\mid\\mathcal D)\\bigr]\n \\le CC_\\omega R^{-c_g},\\qquad\n c_g=\\frac12\\left(\\frac{\\log2}{\\log3}-\\frac12\\right)>0.$$ Both estimates hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the same route construction in reverse order. Finally, if $\\mathcal M$ is the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\\label{eq:route-rejection}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal M\\setminus\\mathcal E)\n \\le K^3(Ap)^{\\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\\rfloor}+CR^3e^{-cR},$$ where $A$ is fixed and $Ap_0<1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-long-product",
        "exact_quote": "Conditional on $\\mathcal D$ and the paths outside $U=S\\cup T$, the two likelihoods satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n S\\cap T=\\varnothing:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le \\mathbb E[h_{w'j_1}h_{w'j_2}\\mid U^c]\n   +Ce^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}\n       \\bigl(B_{ST}+C_xJ_*b_D(1+t^{-3/2})\\bigr),\n       \\label{eq:long-product}\\\\\n |S\\cap T|=q\\ge1:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le e^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}G_D^q.\n       \\label{eq:long-product-overlap}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-long-product",
          "exact_quote": "Conditional on $\\mathcal D$ and the paths outside $U=S\\cup T$, the two likelihoods satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n S\\cap T=\\varnothing:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le \\mathbb E[h_{w'j_1}h_{w'j_2}\\mid U^c]\n   +Ce^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}\n       \\bigl(B_{ST}+C_xJ_*b_D(1+t^{-3/2})\\bigr),\n       \\label{eq:long-product}\\\\\n |S\\cap T|=q\\ge1:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le e^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}G_D^q.\n       \\label{eq:long-product-overlap}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Conditional on $\\mathcal D$ and the paths outside $U=S\\cup T$, the two likelihoods satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n S\\cap T=\\varnothing:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le \\mathbb E[h_{w'j_1}h_{w'j_2}\\mid U^c]\n   +Ce^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}\n       \\bigl(B_{ST}+C_xJ_*b_D(1+t^{-3/2})\\bigr),\n       \\label{eq:long-product}\\\\\n |S\\cap T|=q\\ge1:\\quad\n \\mathbb E[L_SL_T\\mid U^c]\n &\\le e^{C_xD^{-.19}J_c}G_D^q.\n       \\label{eq:long-product-overlap}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normalizer-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For these conditional reference laws, $$\\label{eq:long-normalizer}\n \\frac{Z_S^-Z_T^-}{Z_U}\n       \\le C^{|S\\cap T|}e^{CB_{ST}}.$$ If $S\\cap T=\\varnothing$, then for any measurable $0\\le F\\le1$ of the latent originals, $$\\label{eq:reference-difference}\n 0\\le \\frac1{Z_U}\\int F(A_S^-A_T^--c_U)\\,\\,\\mathrm dB_U\n          \\le CB_{ST}e^{CB_{ST}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-distant-gap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ tend to infinity through integers, and let $(f_z)_{z\\in(\\mathbb Z/M\\mathbb Z)^3}$ be an array in a Hilbert space. For all sufficiently large $M$, $$\\mathbb E_z\\|f_z-\\bar f\\|^2\\le C\\mathbb E_{z,h}\\|f_{z+h}-f_z\\|^2$$ with an absolute constant $C$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rms-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite nonnegative array $(y_i)$, with uniform independent indices $i,i'$, one has $$\\label{eq:rms-fourth-inequality}\n \\left(\\sqrt{\\mathbb E_i y_i^2}-\\mathbb E_i y_i\\right)^2\n \\le\\frac12\\mathbb E_{i,i'}\n          \\frac{|y_i-y_{i'}|^4}{y_i^2+y_{i'}^2},$$ where the quotient is zero when its denominator vanishes. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:square-root-quotient}\n |\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\le\\frac{|y-z|^4}{y^2+z^2}\n \\le8|\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\qquad(y,z\\ge0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rms-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite nonnegative array $(y_i)$, with uniform independent indices $i,i'$, one has $$\\label{eq:rms-fourth-inequality}\n \\left(\\sqrt{\\mathbb E_i y_i^2}-\\mathbb E_i y_i\\right)^2\n \\le\\frac12\\mathbb E_{i,i'}\n          \\frac{|y_i-y_{i'}|^4}{y_i^2+y_{i'}^2},$$ where the quotient is zero when its denominator vanishes. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:square-root-quotient}\n |\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\le\\frac{|y-z|^4}{y^2+z^2}\n \\le8|\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\qquad(y,z\\ge0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a finite nonnegative array $(y_i)$, with uniform independent indices $i,i'$, one has $$\\label{eq:rms-fourth-inequality}\n \\left(\\sqrt{\\mathbb E_i y_i^2}-\\mathbb E_i y_i\\right)^2\n \\le\\frac12\\mathbb E_{i,i'}\n          \\frac{|y_i-y_{i'}|^4}{y_i^2+y_{i'}^2},$$ where the quotient is zero when its denominator vanishes. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:square-root-quotient}\n |\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\le\\frac{|y-z|^4}{y^2+z^2}\n \\le8|\\sqrt y-\\sqrt z|^4\\qquad(y,z\\ge0).$$",
      "explanation": "For a nonnegative finite array, the discrepancy between its root mean square and arithmetic mean is controlled by weighted fourth differences. The companion square-root comparison allows these differences to be telescoped along cell paths.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible interaction potential** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $v:\mathbb R^3\to[0,\infty)$ be bounded, measurable, radial, and compactly supported, and suppose that $v$ is nonzero on a set of positive measure.
- **Physical scattering length** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its scattering length $a_v>0$ is determined by the radial solution of $$(-\Delta+\tfrac12v)f=0, \qquad f(x)=1-\frac{a_v}{\|x\|} \quad\text{for sufficiently large }\|x\|.$$
- **Periodic many-body Hamiltonian** ([source](#pm-h-3)): On the torus $\Lambda_L=(\mathbb R/L\mathbb Z)^3$, set $$v_L(x)=\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^3}v(x+Lz), \qquad H^v_{N,L}=\sum_{j=1}^N(-\Delta_j) +\sum_{i<j}v_L(x_i-x_j),$$ acting on $L^2_{\mathrm{sym}}(\Lambda_L^N)$ with periodic boundary conditions.
- **Ground-state density matrices** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $\mathcal G^v_{N,L}$ for the positive trace-one operators supported on its lowest eigenspace.
- **Constant-mode occupation fraction** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $\Gamma\in\mathcal G^v_{N,L}$, define $$\gamma^{(1)}_\Gamma=N\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{2,\ldots,N}\Gamma, \qquad u_0=L^{-3/2}, \qquad B_\Gamma=\frac{\langle u_0,\gamma^{(1)}_\Gamma u_0\rangle}{N}.$$
- **Scaled variables** ([source](#eq:scaled-variables)): $$\label{eq:scaled-variables} \begin{gathered} K=L/\sigma,\qquad V=K^3,\qquad D=N/V,\qquad a=a_v/\sigma,\\ \alpha=Da,\qquad r=R_v/\sigma,\qquad W(y)=\sigma^2v(\sigma y). \end{gathered}$$ The scaled Hamiltonian has kinetic energy $-\Delta$ and pair potential $W$, periodized on the torus of side $K$. Its normalized positive ground function is denoted by $\Phi$, and $$\mu(\,\mathrm dX)=\Phi(X)^2\,\mathrm dX.$$ Integrals in all particle coordinates use Lebesgue measure. We write $\langle O\rangle_\psi=\langle\psi,O\psi\rangle$; this notation does not normalize a vector that is not already normalized.
- **Ground-state position measure** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Its normalized positive ground function is denoted by $\Phi$, and $$\mu(\,\mathrm dX)=\Phi(X)^2\,\mathrm dX.$$
- **Cellwise projections and second quantization** ([source](#eq:cell-projections)): $$\label{eq:cell-projections} W_l=1-P_l,\qquad \mathrm d\Gamma(O)=\sum_{i=1}^N O_i.$$ Thus $W_l$ is a projection, whereas $W$ without a subscript is the pair potential. Averages over cells or cell pairs are uniform unless a different measure is specified.
- **Ground-state path measure** ([source](#eq:ground-path-law)): For $0<T\le1$, consider independent torus Brownian motions on $[-T,T]$ with generator $\Delta$ and initial Lebesgue measure. Weight their joint law by $$\label{eq:ground-path-law} e^{2TE_N}\Phi(\omega(-T))\Phi(\omega(T)) \prod_{i<k}\exp\left[-\int_{-T}^T W(\omega_i(q)-\omega_k(q))\,\mathrm dq\right].$$ Here and below the pair potential in torus expressions is periodized. The Feynman–Kac formula makes <a href="#eq:ground-path-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ground-path-law">[eq:ground-path-law]</a> a probability measure with marginal $\mu$ at every time. Restrictions to shorter slabs are consistent, by the ground-state eigenfunction identity.
- **Low and high excitation bands** ([source](#eq:energy-bands)): $$\label{eq:energy-bands} J=Q^{100},\qquad n_+=\sum_{p\ne0}a_p^*a_p,\qquad n_L=\sum_{0<\|p\|\le J/E}a_p^*a_p,\qquad n_H=n_+-n_L,\qquad M\asymp Dv_bQ^{-400}.$$ Take $M$ integral. We refer to $0<\|p\|\le J/E$ as the low band and the remaining nonzero modes as the high band. All power inequalities below hold if $u$ is sufficiently small, for example $u<10^{-9}$ and sufficiently small relative to $c_*$. Further decreases are harmless.
- **Bogoliubov mode transformation** ([source](#eq:energy-bogoliubov)): Let $\varphi$ be the scattering function of $W$, and set $$\omega=1-\varphi,\qquad g=W\varphi,\qquad m=g\omega.$$ The maximum principle and the radial scattering equation give $0\le\varphi\le1$, $\widehat g(0)=8\pi a$ and $\\|m\\|_1\le C/D$. For $p\ne0$ put $$\label{eq:energy-tau} S=E^{-2}(n_++Jn_H),\qquad \tau_p=p^2-E^{-2}\bigl(1+J\mathbf 1_{\{p\ \text{high}\}}\bigr).$$ Thus $\tau_p\asymp p^2$. For the constant mode use coherent vectors $\|z\rangle=e^{-\|z\|^2/2}\sum_{q\ge0}z^q\|q\rangle/\sqrt{q!}$, measure $\,\mathrm d\lambda(z)=\pi^{-1}\,\mathrm d^2z$, and coherent projections $\psi_z=\langle z\|\psi\rangle$ onto the excitation Fock space. For $\|z\|^2\le2n$ define $$\rho_z=\|z\|^2/v_b,\qquad t_p=\rho_z\widehat g(p),\qquad D_p=(\tau_p^2+2t_p\tau_p)^{1/2}.$$ These roots are positive: $\|t_p\|\le C$, while $\widehat g(p)\ge0$ on every fixed momentum ball for large $D$, by positivity and shrinking support. Large $p$ are controlled by $\tau_p\asymp p^2$. After rotating excitation modes by the phase of $z$, the change of variables used below diagonalizes the one-mode quadratic form $$(\tau_p+t_p)a_p^*a_p+\tfrac12t_p(a_p^2+a_p^{*2}).$$ Specifically, define $b_p$ through $$\label{eq:energy-bogoliubov} \begin{gathered} a_p=U_pb_p+V_pb_p^*,\qquad U_p>0,\quad U_p^2=1+V_p^2,\\ V_p^2=\tfrac12\left(\frac{\tau_p+t_p}{D_p}-1\right),\qquad 2U_pV_p=-t_p/D_p. \end{gathered}$$ With this choice, $$(\tau_p+t_p)a_p^*a_p+\tfrac12t_p(a_p^2+a_p^{*2}) =D_pb_p^*b_p+\tfrac12(D_p-\tau_p-t_p).$$ The formulas with $z=0$ have $U_p=1,V_p=0$.
- **Deletion measure** ([source](#eq:palm-density)): Proposition <a href="#prop:local-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:local-energy">5</a> determines the occupation outside the $R$-cell constants. We now prepare comparisons that will control the remaining variation between cells. For a cell $v$ of $\mathcal C_d$ or $\mathcal C_R$, define a measure on the remaining $N-1$ coordinates $Y'$ by $$\label{eq:palm-density} P_v(\,\mathrm dY')=s_v(Y')^2\,\mathrm dY',\qquad s_v(Y')=\left(\frac V{\|v\|}\int_v\Phi(y,Y')^2\,\,\mathrm dy\right)^{1/2}.$$ Translation invariance makes the first particle uniform on the torus. Thus $P_v$ is the law obtained by conditioning that particle to lie in $v$ and then forgetting its position. We call it the deletion measure for $v$.
- **Auxiliary soft-interaction check** ([source](#eq:soft-check)): Independently flag each label with probability $f_0$, and let $I$ be the set of flagged eligible labels. We expose the two endpoint configurations, all flags, membership in $I$, and the complete paths of labels outside $I$. We call those paths *frozen*. A further augmentation turns the soft interaction into an exact indicator constraint. Set $$a_{ik}(\omega)=\exp\left\{-\int_{-t}^t W(\omega_i(q)-\omega_k(q))\,\,\mathrm dq\right\}.$$ In the Feynman–Kac density, replace each factor $a_{ik}(\omega)$ by integration over $u_{ik}\in[0,1]$ of the indicator $$\label{eq:soft-check} \mathbf 1_{\{u_{ik}\le a_{ik}(\omega)\}}.$$ Thus the joint density is the original endpoint-weighted free-path density times these indicators and Lebesgue measure in all $u_{ik}$. Its path marginal is unchanged. Equivalently, given the paths, one may sample $u_{ik}$ uniformly on $[0,a_{ik}(\omega)]$. These variables are not independent uniforms on $[0,1]$ after conditioning on the paths. Expose all of them, and denote the entire exposed datum by $\mathcal D$.
- **Normalized flagged count and tag scale** ([source](#pm-h-27)): For a cell $v$ of $\mathcal C_d$ or $\mathcal C_R$, define $$T_v=f_0D\|v\|,\qquad h_{vi}=\mathbf 1_{\{X_i\in v\}},\qquad A_v=\frac1{T_v}\sum_{i\text{ flagged}}h_{vi}.$$
- **Centered conditional likelihood** ([source](#eq:local-centering)): Include the second tag indicator but omit its divisor $T_{w'}$. On admissible output paths the left likelihood is $$\label{eq:local-likelihood} L_S(z;\eta)=\int c_S(o;\eta)h_{w'i_m}(o) k_S(o,\eta,z)B_S(\,\mathrm do).$$ It obeys $$\label{eq:local-centering} F_S=L_S-h_{w'i_m},\qquad \int F_S\,\,\mathrm d\lambda_S(\cdot\mid\eta)=0,$$ and $$\label{eq:local-likelihood-bound} 0\le L_S\le C_xD^{C\sqrt x}.$$ These assertions remain valid after deleting specified outside checks consistently from the reference, the original-path integral, and all insertion tests and their normalizing probabilities.

For $p\\ne0$ put $$\\label{eq:energy-tau}\n S=E^{-2}(n_++Jn_H),\\qquad\n \\tau_p=p^2-E^{-2}\\bigl(1+J\\mathbf 1_{\\{p\\ \\text{high}\\}}\\bigr).$$ Thus $\\tau_p\\asymp p^2$. For the constant mode use coherent vectors $|z\\rangle=e^{-|z|^2/2}\\sum_{q\\ge0}z^q|q\\rangle/\\sqrt{q!}$, measure $\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda(z)=\\pi^{-1}\\,\\mathrm d^2z$, and coherent projections $\\psi_z=\\langle z|\\psi\\rangle$ onto the excitation Fock space. For $|z|^2\\le2n$ define $$\\rho_z=|z|^2/v_b,\\qquad t_p=\\rho_z\\widehat g(p),\\qquad\n D_p=(\\tau_p^2+2t_p\\tau_p)^{1/2}.$$ These roots are positive: $|t_p|\\le C$, while $\\widehat g(p)\\ge0$ on every fixed momentum ball for large $D$, by positivity and shrinking support. Large $p$ are controlled by $\\tau_p\\asymp p^2$. After rotating excitation modes by the phase of $z$, the change of variables used below diagonalizes the one-mode quadratic form $$(\\tau_p+t_p)a_p^*a_p+\\tfrac12t_p(a_p^2+a_p^{*2}).$$ Specifically, define $b_p$ through $$\\label{eq:energy-bogoliubov}\n\\begin{gathered}\n a_p=U_pb_p+V_pb_p^*,\\qquad U_p>0,\\quad U_p^2=1+V_p^2,\\\\\n V_p^2=\\tfrac12\\left(\\frac{\\tau_p+t_p}{D_p}-1\\right),\\qquad\n 2U_pV_p=-t_p/D_p.\n\\end{gathered}$$ With this choice, $$(\\tau_p+t_p)a_p^*a_p+\\tfrac12t_p(a_p^2+a_p^{*2})\n  =D_pb_p^*b_p+\\tfrac12(D_p-\\tau_p-t_p).$$ The formulas with $z=0$ have $U_p=1,V_p=0$.",
      "interpretation": "The canonical transformation diagonalizes each quadratic excitation mode and identifies its vacuum occupation.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Bogoliubov mode transformation",
      "scope": "Coherent parameters and nonconstant Neumann modes in the sharp box estimate."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "eq:palm-density",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:palm-density",
          "exact_quote": "Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-energy\">5</a> determines the occupation outside the $R$-cell constants. We now prepare comparisons that will control the remaining variation between cells. For a cell $v$ of $\\mathcal C_d$ or $\\mathcal C_R$, define a measure on the remaining $N-1$ coordinates $Y'$ by $$\\label{eq:palm-density}\n P_v(\\,\\mathrm dY')=s_v(Y')^2\\,\\mathrm dY',\\qquad\n s_v(Y')=\\left(\\frac V{|v|}\\int_v\\Phi(y,Y')^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dy\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Translation invariance makes the first particle uniform on the torus. Thus $P_v$ is the law obtained by conditioning that particle to lie in $v$ and then forgetting its position. We call it the deletion measure for $v$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Proposition <a href=\"#prop:local-energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:local-energy\">5</a> determines the occupation outside the $R$-cell constants. Thus $P_v$ is the law obtained by conditioning that particle to lie in $v$ and then forgetting its position. We call it the deletion measure for $v$.",
      "interpretation": "Condition a distinguished particle to lie in a cell and forget its position. The resulting bath law has a square-root density given by the cell root mean square of the ground function.",

          "exact_quote": "Independently flag each label with probability $f_0$, and let $I$ be the set of flagged eligible labels. A further augmentation turns the soft interaction into an exact indicator constraint. Set $$a_{ik}(\\omega)=\\exp\\left\\{-\\int_{-t}^t\n                   W(\\omega_i(q)-\\omega_k(q))\\,\\,\\mathrm dq\\right\\}.$$ In the Feynman–Kac density, replace each factor $a_{ik}(\\omega)$ by integration over $u_{ik}\\in[0,1]$ of the indicator $$\\label{eq:soft-check}\n                    \\mathbf 1_{\\{u_{ik}\\le a_{ik}(\\omega)\\}}.$$ Thus the joint density is the original endpoint-weighted free-path density times these indicators and Lebesgue measure in all $u_{ik}$. Its path marginal is unchanged. Equivalently, given the paths, one may sample $u_{ik}$ uniformly on $[0,a_{ik}(\\omega)]$. These variables are not independent uniforms on $[0,1]$ after conditioning on the paths. Expose all of them, and denote the entire exposed datum by $\\mathcal D$.",

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-bogoliubov-depletion-coefficient | Bogoliubov depletion coefficient | = | $\frac8{3\sqrt\pi}$ | The physical interaction remains fixed throughout both limits. |
| q-physical-base-length | \sigma_* | = | $B_0\rho^{-1/2}$ | The base constant depends on the fixed physical potential. |
| q-scaled-base-density | D_* | = | $B_0^3\rho^{-1/2}$ | The physical density varies after the thermodynamic limit. |
| q-local-count-variance | \mathbb E_{A\in\mathcal C_R}\langle(q_A-1)^2\rangle_\Phi | ≤ | $CD^{-1-w/6}$ | Constants are uniform in the fixed compact positive window for the scaled interaction parameter. |
| q-fine-count-fourth-moment | \mathbb E_{A\in\mathcal C_d}\langle(q_A-1)^4\rangle_\Phi | ≤ | $C_jD^{-1-u/6}$ | Constants may depend on the fixed fine-partition parameter. |
| q-mesoscopic-depletion-density | V^{-1}\langle\mathrm d\Gamma(W_R)\rangle_\Phi | = | $\frac8{3\sqrt\pi}\alpha^{3/2}+o(1)$ | Thermodynamic limit at fixed physical density, followed by dilution. |
| q-lee-huang-yang-energy-coefficient | c_{\rm L} | = | $128/(15\sqrt\pi)$ | The scattering length is held fixed when differentiating the energy density. |
| q-energy-surplus-budget | Each retained positive quantity after summing sectors and boxes | ≤ | $CVQ^{-1/5}$ | The box scale and dilute exponents satisfy the energy hierarchy. |
| q-single-site-bad-probability | \mathbb P(z\text{ is bad}) | ≤ | $C_xD^{-1-1/8}$ | Thresholds are uniform in neighborhood position and total volume. |
| q-adjacent-lift-fourth-moment | Averaged fourth-moment errors of the adjacent deletion lifts | ≤ | $C_jD^{-1-u/8}$ | The fine-partition parameter is fixed during both principal limits. |
| q-route-encounter-decay-exponent | c_g | = | $\frac12\left(\frac{\log2}{\log3}-\frac12\right)$ | The geometric exponent is independent of the path exponent; contact parameters remain fixed. |
| q-distant-rms-variance | \mathbb E_{v\in\mathcal C_R}\\|s_v-\bar s\\|^2 | = | $o(D^{-1})+o_K(1)$ | The thermodynamic remainder is removed before the dilute remainder. |

A random datum $\\mathcal D$ determines a bad set $\\mathcal B\\subset\\mathcal L_K$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P(A\\subset\\mathcal B)\\le p^{|A|}\\qquad(A\\subset\\mathcal L_K).$$ Fix an integer contact radius $r_c\\ge1$, an integer $q\\ge0$, and $c_Q>0$. Let $Q$ be a projected lattice cube of side at most $c_QR$, and let $z$ be a site such that every $y\\in Q$ has forward first-coordinate displacement from $z$ in $[.29K,.46K]$. There are constants $p_0,\\kappa,c,C,C_0>0$ and $R_0$, depending only on the fixed parameters, with the following properties when $R\\ge R_0$, $K\\ge C_0R$, and $p\\le p_0$.*\n\n*Put $H=\\lfloor R^{1/2}\\rfloor$. There is an event $\\mathcal E$, determined by $\\mathcal D,z,Q$, on which one can sample simple good nearest-neighbor routes $\\pi_y$ from $z$ to every $y\\in Q$. Conditional on $\\mathcal D$, different samples use independent fresh randomness. The event $\\mathcal E$ includes the requirement that the $10R$-neighborhoods of $z$ and $Q$ contain no bad sites.*\n\n\n\n\n*For $\\pi=(v_0,\\ldots,v_m)$ starting at $z$, put $$a(\\pi)=\\max\\{i:d_\\infty(v_i,z)\\le H\\},\\qquad\n \\pi^{\\rm ch}=\\{v_i:i>a(\\pi)\\}.$$ The initial portion $(v_0,\\ldots,v_{a(\\pi)})$ has at most $CH$ vertices. The analogous portion read backwards from the terminal site also has at most $CH$ vertices, and these two portions are disjoint. Let $\\pi^{\\rm ch,q}$ contain the vertices whose path index is within $q$ of an index in $\\pi^{\\rm ch}$. For independent samples $\\pi_y,\\pi_{y'}$, set $$J_c=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y}\\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}},\\qquad\n J_*=\\sum_{v\\in\\pi_y^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\sum_{v'\\in\\pi_{y'}^{\\rm ch,q}}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{d_\\infty(v,v')\\le r_c\\}}.$$ Uniformly in the specified endpoints, $$\\label{eq:route-exponential}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\bigl[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E}\n       \\mathbb E_{\\rm routes}(e^{\\kappa J_c}\\mid\\mathcal D)\\bigr]\\le C.$$ For every deterministic probability mass function $\\omega$ on $Q\\times Q$ with $\\omega(y,y')\\le C_\\omega R^{-6}$, $$\\label{eq:route-rare}\n \\sum_{y,y'\\in Q}\\omega(y,y')\n   \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal D}\\bigl[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E}\n       \\mathbb P_{\\rm routes}(J_*>0\\mid\\mathcal D)\\bigr]\n \\le CC_\\omega R^{-c_g},\\qquad\n c_g=\\frac12\\left(\\frac{\\log2}{\\log3}-\\frac12\\right)>0.$$ Both estimates hold with a common terminal endpoint, reading the same route construction in reverse order. Finally, if $\\mathcal M$ is the stated bad-free margin event, then $$\\label{eq:route-rejection}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathcal M\\setminus\\mathcal E)\n \\le K^3(Ap)^{\\lfloor K/(10(r_c+2))\\rfloor}+CR^3e^{-cR},$$ where $A$ is fixed and $Ap_0<1$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-density-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-density-input) / [proof](#pm-h-9): Import localized density and occupation tails with temperature-uniform volume thresholds, then pass canonical Gibbs states to the finite-volume ground state. -> Upper and lower local density-tail inputs.
- **step-scaled-count-tails** [claim](#stmt-lem-count-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-count-tails): Cover cell unions by enlarged base cubes and extract a separated subcollection by bounded coloring. -> Scaled exponential upper and simultaneous lower count tails.
- **step-free-path-domination** [claim](#stmt-lem-path-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-path-domination): Delete nonnegative incident interactions, bound the remaining semigroup, and use reversal symmetry to compare slice norms. -> Free-path domination and the ingredients for local pair bounds.
- **step-rough-box-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-rough) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-rough): Apply radial scattering replacement in small cubes, use a kinetic reserve for Temple's inequality, sum convex count penalties, and recover a fixed kinetic form. -> Rough scalar lower bound with retained kinetic and count penalties.
- **step-sharp-box-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-sharp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-sharp): Localize the excitation number, split scattering squares, resolve the constant mode coherently, diagonalize the quadratic form, and cancel the high-frequency cubic contraction against the retained interaction term. -> Sharp local energy scalar with positive excitation and quasiparticle remainders.
- **step-energy-surplus** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-surplus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-surplus): Compare every number and excitation sector with the affine energy tangent, retain penalties, and sum localization and wall errors. -> A common budget for count penalties and positive excitation forms.
- **step-local-energy-outputs** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-energy) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Use the surplus to control count moments, remove clipping with density tails, compare occupation observables with vacuum traces, and evaluate the limiting momentum integrals. -> Coarse variance, fine fourth moments, mesoscopic depletion, and fine occupation control.
- **step-conditional-indicator-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-path-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-path-law): Augment each interaction factor by an auxiliary indicator and disintegrate after exposing endpoints, eligibility, outside paths, and auxiliary variables. -> Exact conditional indicator-weighted Euclidean bridge law.
- **step-good-data** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-good-data): Transfer Gaussian motion bounds through path domination, estimate close endpoint pairs, apply independent flag tails, and control fine spatial counts by exponential moments. -> Uniform good-site and full-path probability bounds.
- **step-collision-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-collision-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collision-cost): Orient bridge pieces from separated endpoints, exclude early collisions using motion cutoffs, and sum Gaussian mesh-point bounds; integrate the midpoint singularity when averaging pins. -> Endpoint and midpoint collision costs uniform in auxiliary checks.
- **step-uniform-insertion** [claim](#stmt-prop-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-insertion): Sum sparse flagged collision costs by endpoint counts and dense frozen collision costs by weighted mesh counts, then include individual cutoff failures. -> Uniform positive insertion probabilities, including shared-midpoint and pinned trials.
- **step-bridge-domination** [claim](#stmt-cor-bridge-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-bridge-domination): Delete selected labels and insert them successively, comparing their conditional normalization factors. -> Product free-bridge domination for selected conditional marginals.
- **step-flagged-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-counts): Condition on midpoint counts, use binomial moments, and bound eligibility omissions with motion and endpoint pair estimates. -> Normalized flagged-count moments, tag omission, and relative-count product control.
- **step-centered-likelihood** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-likelihood) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-likelihood): Construct paired local transport kernels, cancel the conditional reference normalizer on admissible outputs, and use probability-kernel mass to prove centering. -> Centered likelihood and density control, also under consistent check deletion.
- **step-local-switches** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-switches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-switches): Switch checks consistently in references and proposal normalizations; expand finite differences and integrate singleton leaves supplying forced collision factors. -> Single-switch and mixed-switch conditional bounds.
- **step-adjacent-palm** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-palm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-palm): Expand the averaged centered fourth moment, cancel isolated lists, pay for label identifications and switched collisions, then project the common measure by conditional Jensen. -> Adjacent deletion-density fourth-moment comparison.
- **step-route-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-route-input) / [proof](#pm-h-32): Import the good-route construction and verify the joint bad-site law, endpoint geometry, contact radius, and retained margin conditions. -> Independent route samples with exponential contacts and rare changed-portion encounters.
- **step-long-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-long-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-long-mass): Retain original end portions, generate middle pairs with unit mass, use capped pin denominators, and bound omissions and original midpoint separation failures. -> Common long-transport mass close to the original tag-product mass.
- **step-normalizer-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalizer-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalizer-comparison): Restore removed cross checks successively and use selected-label domination; delete and reinsert shared labels when changed sets overlap. -> Separate-to-union normalizer ratio and disjoint reference difference bounds.
- **step-long-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-long-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalizer-comparison-2): Compare generation protocols after deleting cross checks, retain latent pin sources for inverse-distance averaging, and bound only shared primary marginals in the overlap case. -> Conditional likelihood-product bounds without a route-length expense.
- **step-distant-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-distant-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distant-gap): Apply the finite Fourier transform and bound each nonconstant displacement multiplier away from zero. -> Volume-independent distant-pair variance control.
- **step-rms-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-rms-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rms-comparison): Apply Cauchy–Schwarz to weighted fourth differences and factor the difference through square roots, treating vanishing denominators separately. -> Root-mean-square discrepancy and square-root quotient comparisons.
- **step-assemble-depletion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rms-comparison-2): Average labels and independent routes to bound distant likelihood errors, project to deletion densities, use the distant gap, replace coarse root mean squares by arithmetic means, and restore physical units. -> The constant-orbital depletion asymptotic in the stipulated order of limits.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-rms-fourth-comparison**: For a finite nonnegative array, the squared discrepancy between its root mean square and arithmetic mean is bounded by the averaged fourth differences divided by the sum of squared entries, with the stated zero-denominator convention.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-signed-array**: Apply the root mean square comparison to arrays containing negative entries. -> The proof uses nonnegativity of the arithmetic mean when comparing the squared expressions.
- **anti-zero-denominator**: Evaluate every pair quotient by ordinary division. -> A pair of zero entries produces an undefined quotient.
- **anti-finite-depletion-certificate**: Treat successful finite-array checks as a certificate of the ground-state depletion asymptotic. -> The array inequality does not verify the interacting ground state, the deletion comparisons, or the ordered thermodynamic and dilute limits.
- **anti-universal-from-vectors**: Infer that finitely many accepted rational arrays prove the inequality for every real nonnegative array. -> The executed vectors cover only their declared inputs; the universal conclusion requires the analytical argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The root mean square comparison is a finite-array result. Its rational specialization computes the arithmetic mean, mean square, and averaged pair bound exactly, and decides the inequality by a sign-aware squaring comparison. This verifies an auxiliary inequality rather than the thermodynamic depletion theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON nonempty array of nonnegative rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Every entry is an exact rational number and is nonnegative.
# OUTPUTS: JSON exact mean_square, mean, pair_bound, and Boolean holds.
# TIME: Enumeration of all ordered index pairs with exact rational arithmetic.
# SPACE: Storage of the input array and rational accumulators.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fractions; square roots are eliminated with a sign guard.
# FAILURE: Invalid input exits unsuccessfully without a mathematical output.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def evaluate(raw):
    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw or any(not isinstance(s, str) for s in raw):
        raise ValueError('nonempty rational-string array required')
    y = [Fraction(s) for s in raw]
    if any(v < 0 for v in y):
        raise ValueError('nonnegative entries required')
    n = len(y)
    A = sum((v * v for v in y), Fraction(0)) / n
    b = sum(y, Fraction(0)) / n
    pair_sum = Fraction(0)
    for left in y:
        for right in y:
            denominator = left * left + right * right
            if denominator:
                pair_sum += (left - right) ** 4 / denominator
    R = pair_sum / (2 * n * n)
    d = A + b * b - R
    holds = d <= 0 or d * d <= 4 * b * b * A
    return {'mean_square': str(A), 'mean': str(b), 'pair_bound': str(R), 'holds': holds}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    try:
        result = evaluate(json.load(sys.stdin))
    except Exception:
        sys.exit(2)
    print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))
```

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: A JSON nonempty array of nonnegative rational strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Every entry is an exact rational number and is nonnegative.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON exact mean_square, mean, pair_bound, and Boolean holds.\n# TIME: Enumeration of all ordered index pairs with exact rational arithmetic.\n# SPACE: Storage of the input array and rational accumulators.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact fractions; square roots are eliminated with a sign guard.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input exits unsuccessfully without a mathematical output.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef evaluate(raw):\n    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw or any(not isinstance(s, str) for s in raw):\n        raise ValueError('nonempty rational-string array required')\n    y = [Fraction(s) for s in raw]\n    if any(v < 0 for v in y):\n        raise ValueError('nonnegative entries required')\n    n = len(y)\n    A = sum((v * v for v in y), Fraction(0)) / n\n    b = sum(y, Fraction(0)) / n\n    pair_sum = Fraction(0)\n    for left in y:\n        for right in y:\n            denominator = left * left + right * right\n            if denominator:\n                pair_sum += (left - right) ** 4 / denominator\n    R = pair_sum / (2 * n * n)\n    d = A + b * b - R\n    holds = d <= 0 or d * d <= 4 * b * b * A\n    return {'mean_square': str(A), 'mean': str(b), 'pair_bound': str(R), 'holds': holds}\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        result = evaluate(json.load(sys.stdin))\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(2)\n    print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "{\"mean_square\":\"0\",\"mean\":\"0\",\"pair_bound\":\"0\",\"holds\":true}",
        "input": "[\"0\"]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"mean_square\":\"4\",\"mean\":\"2\",\"pair_bound\":\"0\",\"holds\":true}",
        "input": "[\"2\"]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"mean_square\":\"4\",\"mean\":\"2\",\"pair_bound\":\"0\",\"holds\":true}",
        "input": "[\"2\",\"2\"]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"mean_square\":\"2\",\"mean\":\"1\",\"pair_bound\":\"1\",\"holds\":true}",
        "input": "[\"0\",\"2\"]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"mean_square\":\"3\",\"mean\":\"1\",\"pair_bound\":\"2\",\"holds\":true}",
        "input": "[\"0\",\"0\",\"3\"]"

        "input": "[\"0\",\"1/2\",\"3/2\"]"

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-admissible-interaction-class** (hypothesis): The depletion theorem requires a fixed bounded, measurable, radial, nonnegative interaction of finite range that is nonzero on a set of positive measure. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem directly to attractive, unbounded, nonradial, or infinite-range interactions.', 'Change the physical interaction during the limiting procedure.']
- **limit-periodic-ground-state-model** (formal_scope): The occupation observable belongs to bosonic ground states on the periodic spatial torus. The theorem does not assert a dynamical, trapped-system, or positive-temperature depletion law. Blocked use: ['Use the ground-state asymptotic as a time-evolution rule.', 'Apply the same conclusion to trapped systems or thermal states without a separate theorem.']
- **limit-ordered-thermodynamic-and-dilute-limits** (hypothesis): The thermodynamic limsup at fixed density precedes dilution. Fine partition parameters remain fixed during these limits, and their thresholds may depend on the chosen partition. Blocked use: ['Replace the ordered limits by a coupled spatial and dilute limit.', 'Assume estimates uniform in a changing fine partition parameter.']
- **limit-no-finite-density-error-certificate** (unknown): An explicit density threshold and a finite-volume convergence rate are ABSENT from the supplied result. The asymptotic coefficient alone does not certify a tolerance for a specified finite computation. Blocked use: ['Turn the leading depletion expression into a certified finite-density stopping criterion.', 'Infer an explicit admissible density or volume from the asymptotic theorem.']
- **limit-energy-does-not-determine-global-occupation** (formal_scope): An accurate scalar energy expansion alone does not determine occupation of the global constant orbital. The proof also needs the retained positive forms and spatial deletion comparisons. Blocked use: ['Discard positive excitation remainders and claim the depletion conclusion from the scalar energy expansion.', 'Identify mesoscopic occupation with global constant-mode occupation before controlling variation between cells.']
- **limit-density-tail-input-conditions** (hypothesis): The imported density tails require the stated density windows, smooth majorant conditions or permitted enlargements, and separation of cubes for the lower-tail assertion. Temperature-uniform volume thresholds enable passage to the ground state. Blocked use: ['Apply the lower-tail estimate to arbitrary overlapping cubes without the required separation.', 'Apply the smooth-count estimate without checking its gradient and support conditions.', 'Replace temperature-uniform thresholds by thresholds that diverge as temperature decreases.']

A weight without that symmetry must first be enlarged using its reversal. - **limit-energy-localization-sectors** (hypothesis): The rough and sharp box estimates have different particle-number and excitation-support restrictions. Their use outside those restrictions requires the separate grouping and high-piece arguments. Blocked use: ['Apply the sharp estimate to an unrestricted excitation vector.', 'Apply a middle-sector bound to all particle-number sectors without the outlier analysis.']

The argument does not supply a pointwise comparison based on radial monotonicity.           "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal L_K=(\\mathbb Z/K\\mathbb Z)^3$, let $d_\\infty$ be the periodic maximum distance.
